=== Source references in Blaze UAV ===

MIL-F-8785C (5 November 1980), Military Specification: Flying Qualities of Piloted Airplanes
Public U.S. military specification. Source for the shear and discrete gust equations in
simulation-engine/engine-core/src/wind.rs:1–8 and the low-altitude range in
simulation-engine/engine-core/src/turbulence.rs:1–4. The reference table identifies the
original document and locators at database/reference/physics.v1.json:1669–1670,1678–1693.
https://everyspec.com/MIL-SPECS/MIL-SPECS-MIL-F/MIL-F-8785C_5295/

The turbulence implementation derives filters from spectra and distinguishes the secondary
MathWorks reference at turbulence.rs:1–5 and documentation/plans/phase-6-flight-environment.md:153–168.
Ground-effect equations cite NACA, NASA and published research at
simulation-engine/engine-core/src/ground_effect.rs:1–86.

SplitMix64
The constants and transition in simulation-engine/engine-core/src/turbulence.rs:54–70
cite https://prng.di.unimi.it/splitmix64.c. The upstream licence statement
in splitmix64.c:1–16 reads as follows.

/*  Written in 2015 by Sebastiano Vigna (vigna@acm.org)

To the extent possible under law, the author has dedicated all copyright
and related and neighboring rights to this software to the public domain
worldwide.

Permission to use, copy, modify, and/or distribute this software for any
purpose with or without fee is hereby granted.

THE SOFTWARE IS PROVIDED "AS IS" AND THE AUTHOR DISCLAIMS ALL WARRANTIES
WITH REGARD TO THIS SOFTWARE INCLUDING ALL IMPLIED WARRANTIES OF
MERCHANTABILITY AND FITNESS. IN NO EVENT SHALL THE AUTHOR BE LIABLE FOR
ANY SPECIAL, DIRECT, INDIRECT, OR CONSEQUENTIAL DAMAGES OR ANY DAMAGES
WHATSOEVER RESULTING FROM LOSS OF USE, DATA OR PROFITS, WHETHER IN AN
ACTION OF CONTRACT, NEGLIGENCE OR OTHER TORTIOUS ACTION, ARISING OUT OF OR
IN CONNECTION WITH THE USE OR PERFORMANCE OF THIS SOFTWARE. */

Chromium gamepad tables (BSD-style)
src/features/input/playstation.ts:1,16–20 follows device/gamepad/gamepad_id_list.cc:153–158,
gamepad_id_list.h:114–118, gamepad_standard_mappings_win.cc:50–70,80–101,548–557 and
gamepad_standard_mappings.h:54–72,84–89,99–110. src/features/flight/device-buttons.ts:5 follows
gamepad_standard_mappings.h:54–58,64,69–70. The captured source headers give these notices.
Copyright 2018 The Chromium Authors (gamepad_id_list.cc and gamepad_id_list.h:1).
Copyright 2012 The Chromium Authors (gamepad_standard_mappings_win.cc and gamepad_standard_mappings.h:1).
The Chromium Project entry in LICENSES.chromium.html:94525–94554 contains its BSD terms.
Source and binary redistribution require retaining the copyright, conditions and disclaimer.
https://chromium.googlesource.com/chromium/src/+/main/device/gamepad/

W3C Gamepad
src/features/input/calibration.ts:17 uses the axis indices in the Standard Gamepad
layout. The captured W3C document identifies its Software and Document Notice and License
at w3c-gamepad.html:339–347. The application uses the interface's numeric assignments.
https://w3c.github.io/gamepad/

Parry and Rapier
src/domain/terrain.ts:118–123 and simulation-engine/engine-core/src/environment.rs:274–278,
556–558,587–590,687–690 cite Parry geometry or query behavior. Rapier and Parry declare
Apache-2.0 in rapier3d-f64-0.36.1/Cargo.toml:42 and parry3d-f64-0.31.1/Cargo.toml:40.
Apache-2.0 section 4 calls for a copy of the licence, notices on modified files, retention of
applicable source notices and reproduction of applicable NOTICE attributions when supplied.
The Parry archive separately attributes third-party code in src/utils/vec_map.rs:1–12
and src/transformation/voxelization/voxel_set.rs:1–17 to the Rust Project Developers
(MIT or Apache-2.0) and Khaled Mamou (BSD-3-Clause terms), respectively.

=== Copyleft terms in the Electron distribution's credits ===

FFmpeg (ffmpeg.dll). The FFmpeg credits at LICENSES.chromium.html:26686–26702 describe
LGPL-2.1-or-later and optional GPL-2.0-or-later build parts. LGPL 2.1 sections 4 and 6
require corresponding library source or a qualifying offer, licence and use notices,
and a way to run with a modified library. The combined-work terms must permit modification
for the customer's own use and reverse engineering for debugging those modifications.
The local DLL did not establish whether the optional GPL parts were enabled.

The Mozilla Windows Cert code and Hunspell entries (LICENSES.chromium.html:55088–55095,
33425–33433) carry MPL terms. MPL 2.0 sections 3.1–3.3 require making covered source and
modifications available under MPL, informing recipients how to obtain it, and allow larger
works under other terms. MPL 1.1 sections 3.2 and 3.6 require making covered modifications
available in source form and giving executable recipients an applicable source notice.
The credits cover multiple platforms and include optional or alternate terms. A credits
entry alone does not establish that its code or a particular licence option is in the Windows build.
The credits also include MPL terms for NSPR, the Mozilla Personal Security Manager,
Eigen, and the Symphonia family (LICENSES.chromium.html:55960,54608,24864,87948–92161),
as well as alternate terms for the ISimpleDOM COM interfaces (42369–42380).

A GPL notice for DirectX Shader Compiler is scoped to autoconf/config.guess in the credits
(LICENSES.chromium.html:23513–23517). JSZip has an MIT or GPL-3.0 choice in the credits
(43701–43708). GPL executable-distribution terms require corresponding source and the
applicable GPL terms when the GPL applies. The available evidence does not establish that
the separately licensed build script or an optional GPL configuration ships in this release.
